Visual review of the state predictors

How each method looks next to the physics engine in the clips on the State predictors tabs, rated by eye with the method's name and error hidden. These numbers come from the visuals only. Updated 06 November 2026, 18:43 UTC.

In short

  1. Best on both sets: non-block regression and masked regression (forward only). Simple set: right on 39 and 38 of 50 clips, none implausible. V2: 58 of 99 each, with 4 and 5 implausible. Non-block regression shakes less on V2 (5 clips against 13).
  2. A higher still weight helps non-block regression a little on V2: right on 62 of 99 at weight 1 and 61 at weight 3, against 58 at 0.1, with wrong clips down from 7 to 4 at weight 3. The differences are small.
  3. The calmest good methods are flow without blocks: non-block flow (32 of 50, 40 of 99) and masked flow, forward only (27 of 50, 44 of 99) shake in 0 to 2 clips, but go wrong more often on V2 (about 20 of 99).
  4. Asking one model all the questions costs looks: masked regression falls to 32 of 50 and 38 of 99 (16 shaking on V2), masked flow to 20 of 50 and 33 of 99 (31 wrong on V2).
  5. Chains hold up worst in furnished rooms. Regression chain 5 falls from 30 of 50 to 15 of 99, chain 10 from 26 to 15, chain 3 from 24 to 5. Chain 15 keeps 23 of 99, but 20 are implausible. On V2 furniture, regression chains 5, 10 and 15 are right on 0 of 14.
  6. Flow chains look the worst of the methods that use chains: flow 100M chain 5 is right on 20 of 50 and 12 of 99, wrong on 51 of 99 and shakes in 32. Chain 10 looks about the same as chain 5 when rated side by side, even though its measured test error is lower (0.223 m against 0.261 m).
  7. The first single-block models and diffusion look worst: regression 100M is right on 1 of 50 and 3 of 99, flow 100M on 9 and 10, non-block diffusion on 6 and 8.
  8. By kind of action, for non-block regression on V2: strong on swings (7 of 7), mechanisms (10 of 13) and topples (6 of 10, none wrong); weakest on throws (3 right, 3 wrong of 7). Masked regression, forward only, does best on topples (9 of 10) and forces in flight (7 of 9), and worst on springs and bounces (1 right, 4 wrong of 6).
  9. Scenes that defeat even the best methods on V2: chair throw table 2, elastic bridge 1 and funnel 1 look wrong for all four of the best, and composite 2 for three. Funnel is a family the models never trained on.
  10. Most visible shaking is on furniture: table topple, cabinet tip, chair push, chair throw, crate drop and chair spill. Flow chains shake most, in 31 to 38 of 99 V2 clips.

ball block catchA red ball rolls along the table and knocks a blue block off the edge; both fall by about 1.2 s and rest inside the box on the floor.partial
balloonA blue balloon with a small silver weight and a dark ball both rise off the post, reach the ceiling by about 2 s and rest there side by side.clear
billiardsA tight cluster of blue, red and brown balls is struck and scatters to the lower right; by the end most rest near the right side of the room and one blue ball sits near the bottom.clear
books ball catchThe arm pushes the books on the table, a red ball rolls off the edge by 1.2 s, drops into the red basket by 2 s and stays inside it.partial
bowlingA ball hits a tight group of pins around 1 s; the pins scatter, the ball leaves view by 2 s, and three visible pins rest in a short spread near the group's origin with the blue pin nearly in place.partial
cabinet tip shedA pale cabinet tips onto its back within about 1 s and lies still pointing away from the camera; small items shed to its left and one rolls out at the front right.clear
carrying cartA dark cart with blue wheels rolls left toward two dark blocks, stops just short of them by 2 s, and rests there; the blocks stay still.clear
cascadeThe yellow arm sends a line of small balls and blocks sliding across the floor toward the far wall, ending as a thin spread-out row left of the arm near the back wall.partial

How this was rated · the rating scales, the control and how far to trust the counts
  • What was rated: the clips on the State predictors and State predictors on V2 tabs. Each method's tile next to the physics engine: five moments of the clip, plus where things moved, where the picture trembles and how much it changes over time, all taken from the published video.
  • Hidden from the raters: the method (a random code per clip) and the tile's error number.
  • Scales: right = same objects, same outcome, same timing; partly = same kind of event, visibly off; wrong = a different outcome. Not plausible = an obvious violation seen on its own: continuous shaking, passing through things, hovering, parts coming apart, motion with no cause.
  • Visibly shakes: the rater saw shaking, or the published video's pixels show it: the tile's picture changes faster than about 3 times a second at least twice as much as the physics engine's tile in the same clip. On the clips checked by eye, the tiles this marks show jagged motion paths and the others smooth ones. Shaking smaller than a pixel of these tiles does not show here, so the Physical plausibility block of the State predictors tab, which works on the predicted positions, finds more of it.
  • Control: the physics engine was slipped in under a code in every clip. It was rated right in 158 of 158 clips.
  • How easy to judge: 57 clips clear, 92 partly, 9 too small or dark (left out). The sheets are small: objects of a few pixels can hide small errors.
  • Limits: AI raters looked at frames and motion maps, not the playing video, and one or two clips per family is a small sample, so counts within a kind are rough.

Checks after rating, not used in any number on this page. The tiles' hidden errors rise with the ratings: median 0.084 m for right, 0.27 m for partly and 0.52 m for wrong on the simple set, and 0.16, 0.31 and 0.66 m on V2. The ratings also catch what the error misses: in trampoline, tiles with about 0.05 m error are rated wrong because the bowl passes through the table. The flow chain 10 tiles were rated in a later pass, with two earlier tiles shown again as a reference: 81% got the same rating and 0.7% the opposite one, but that pass was somewhat harsher (flow 100M chain 5 on the simple set: 18 right and 14 wrong the first time, 15 and 21 the second), so chain 10's counts are a little low next to the others. Eleven V2 clips whose camera looked into furniture were rendered again from a clear viewpoint and rated afterwards. Flow 25M and 50M chain 5 are no longer in the simple-set grid and the still-weight runs are not in it, so they have V2 numbers only.
